1	// Box rendering, layout, and hit testing.
2	// Each box knows its world-space position; the camera transform is applied by the canvas.
3	
4	const Boxes = (() => {
5	  const BOX_W         = 180;
6	  const BOX_HEADER    = 36;
7	  const PORT_ROW_H    = 22;
8	  const PORT_R        = 6;   // port dot radius in world units
9	  const MIN_BOX_H     = 80;
10	
11	  const KIND_COLOR = { call: '#4a9eff', data: '#4caf7d' };
12	
13	  // Comparator branch colors: lt=orange, eq=blue, gt=green
14	  const BRANCH_COLOR = { lt: '#ff8c42', eq: '#4a9eff', gt: '#4caf7d' };
15	
16	  // boxes: { [id]: box_data }
17	  let boxes = {};
18	
19	  // {{{ box_height
20	  function box_height(box) {
21	    const n_in  = (box.inputs || []).length;
22	    // Output rows: iterator boxes have N named slots (issue 221);
23	    // comparator active: 3 dots (lt/eq/gt); sink: 0; otherwise 1.
24	    let n_out;
25	    if (box.iterator_outputs) {
26	      n_out = box.iterator_outputs.length;
27	    } else if (box.has_output === false) {
28	      n_out = 0;
29	    } else if (box.comparand && box.comparand !== '') {
30	      n_out = 3;
31	    } else {
32	      n_out = 1;
33	    }
34	    return Math.max(MIN_BOX_H, BOX_HEADER + Math.max(n_in, n_out) * PORT_ROW_H + 8);
35	  }
36	  // }}}
37	
38	  // {{{ port_positions
39	  // Returns world-space {x,y} for each port dot on a box.
40	  // Output side: null-named single dot when no comparand; 'lt'/'eq'/'gt' dots when comparand set.
41	  function port_positions(box) {
42	    const bx = box.ui?.x ?? 0;
43	    const by = box.ui?.y ?? 0;
44	    const h  = box_height(box);
45	    const inputs = box.inputs || [];
46	
47	    const in_pts = inputs.map((p, i) => ({
48	      name: p.name, side: 'input',
49	      x: bx,
50	      y: by + BOX_HEADER + PORT_ROW_H * i + PORT_ROW_H / 2,
51	    }));
52	
53	    let out_pts;
54	    if (box.has_output === false) {
55	      // sink box — function has no return values (issue 226). No output
56	      // port, no wire-grab target, nothing for the inspector to render.
57	      out_pts = [];
58	    } else if (box.iterator_outputs) {
59	      // iterator box (issue 221) — N named output dots stacked down
60	      // the right edge, one per declared slot. Slot names land in the
61	      // `name` field, so the wire's `from_branch` will pick them up
62	      // exactly like a comparator branch name.
63	      out_pts = box.iterator_outputs.map((slot, i) => ({
64	        name: slot, side: 'output',
65	        x: bx + BOX_W,
66	        y: by + BOX_HEADER + PORT_ROW_H * i + PORT_ROW_H / 2,
67	      }));
68	    } else if (box.comparand && box.comparand !== '') {
69	      // three comparator dots
70	      out_pts = ['lt', 'eq', 'gt'].map((branch, i) => ({
71	        name: branch, side: 'output',
72	        x: bx + BOX_W,
73	        y: by + BOX_HEADER + PORT_ROW_H * i + PORT_ROW_H / 2,
74	      }));
75	    } else {
76	      // single output dot, vertically centered in the box
77	      out_pts = [{
78	        name: null, side: 'output',
79	        x: bx + BOX_W,
80	        y: by + h / 2,
81	      }];
82	    }
83	
84	    return { inputs: in_pts, outputs: out_pts };
85	  }
86	  // }}}
87	
88	  // {{{ draw_box
89	  function draw_box(ctx, box, selected) {
90	    const x = box.ui?.x ?? 0;
91	    const y = box.ui?.y ?? 0;
92	    const w = BOX_W;
93	    const h = box_height(box);
94	    const kind  = box.kind || 'call';
95	    const color = KIND_COLOR[kind] || '#888';
96	
97	    // shadow when selected
98	    if (selected) {
99	      ctx.shadowColor = color;
100	      ctx.shadowBlur  = 12;
101	    }
102	
103	    // body
104	    ctx.fillStyle   = '#1e2130';
105	    ctx.strokeStyle = selected ? color : '#3a3f55';
106	    ctx.lineWidth   = selected ? 2 : 1;
107	    ctx.beginPath();
108	    ctx.roundRect(x, y, w, h, 6);
109	    ctx.fill();
110	    ctx.stroke();
111	
112	    ctx.shadowBlur = 0;
113	
114	    // header bar
115	    ctx.fillStyle = color + '33';
116	    ctx.beginPath();
117	    ctx.roundRect(x, y, w, BOX_HEADER, [6, 6, 0, 0]);
118	    ctx.fill();
119	
120	    // label
121	    ctx.fillStyle  = '#e8eaf6';
122	    ctx.font       = 'bold 12px monospace';
123	    ctx.textAlign  = 'left';
124	    ctx.textBaseline = 'middle';
125	    ctx.fillText(box.label || box.id, x + 10, y + BOX_HEADER / 2);
126	
127	    // kind badge (top right)
128	    ctx.fillStyle  = color;
129	    ctx.font       = '9px monospace';
130	    ctx.textAlign  = 'right';
131	    ctx.fillText(kind.toUpperCase(), x + w - 8, y + BOX_HEADER / 2);
132	
133	    // port dots and labels
134	    const { inputs: in_pts, outputs: out_pts } = port_positions(box);
135	
136	    ctx.font      = '10px monospace';
137	    ctx.fillStyle = '#9ea3c0';
138	
139	    in_pts.forEach(p => {
140	      ctx.beginPath();
141	      ctx.arc(p.x, p.y, PORT_R, 0, Math.PI * 2);
142	      ctx.fillStyle   = '#2d3250';
143	      ctx.strokeStyle = '#6c72a0';
144	      ctx.lineWidth   = 1.5;
145	      ctx.fill();
146	      ctx.stroke();
147	
148	      // Port name label rendered into the canvas (issue 224 reverted
149	      // the DOM-overlay experiment — a click-capturing <input> next to
150	      // the dot was capturing drag attempts). Drawn in world space,
151	      // so it scales with zoom. Editing happens in the inspector.
152	      ctx.fillStyle  = '#9ea3c0';
153	      ctx.font       = '10px monospace';
154	      ctx.textAlign  = 'left';
155	      ctx.textBaseline = 'middle';
156	      ctx.fillText(p.name, p.x + PORT_R + 4, p.y);
157	    });
158	
159	    out_pts.forEach(p => {
160	      // comparator branches use distinct colors; single output uses kind color
161	      const dot_color = p.name ? (BRANCH_COLOR[p.name] || color) : color;
162	      ctx.beginPath();
163	      ctx.arc(p.x, p.y, PORT_R, 0, Math.PI * 2);
164	      ctx.fillStyle   = dot_color + '44';
165	      ctx.strokeStyle = dot_color;
166	      ctx.lineWidth   = 1.5;
167	      ctx.fill();
168	      ctx.stroke();
169	
170	      // label branch name ('lt'/'eq'/'gt') to the left of the dot; nothing for single wire
171	      if (p.name) {
172	        ctx.fillStyle  = dot_color;
173	        ctx.font       = '9px monospace';
174	        ctx.textAlign  = 'right';
175	        ctx.textBaseline = 'middle';
176	        ctx.fillText(p.name, p.x - PORT_R - 4, p.y);
177	      }
178	    });
179	  }
180	  // }}}
181	
182	  // {{{ draw_all
183	  function draw_all(ctx, selected_id) {
184	    for (const id in boxes) {
185	      draw_box(ctx, boxes[id], id === selected_id);
186	    }
187	  }
188	  // }}}
189	
190	  // {{{ hit_test_port
191	  // Returns {box_id, port_name, side, x, y} for the port under world point (wx,wy), or null.
192	  // For output dots: port_name is null (single wire) or 'lt'/'eq'/'gt' (comparator).
193	  function hit_test_port(wx, wy) {
194	    for (const id in boxes) {
195	      const { inputs, outputs } = port_positions(boxes[id]);
196	      for (const p of [...inputs, ...outputs]) {
197	        const dx = wx - p.x, dy = wy - p.y;
198	        if (dx*dx + dy*dy <= (PORT_R + 4) * (PORT_R + 4)) {
199	          return { box_id: id, port_name: p.name, side: p.side, x: p.x, y: p.y };
200	        }
201	      }
202	    }
203	    return null;
204	  }
205	  // }}}
206	
207	  // {{{ hit_test_box
208	  // Returns box id if (wx,wy) is inside a box body, or null.
209	  function hit_test_box(wx, wy) {
210	    for (const id in boxes) {
211	      const b = boxes[id];
212	      const x = b.ui?.x ?? 0, y = b.ui?.y ?? 0;
213	      if (wx >= x && wx <= x + BOX_W && wy >= y && wy <= y + box_height(b)) return id;
214	    }
215	    return null;
216	  }
217	  // }}}
218	
219	  // {{{ get_port_world_pos
220	  function get_port_world_pos(box_id, port_name, side) {
221	    const box = boxes[box_id];
222	    if (!box) return null;
223	    const { inputs, outputs } = port_positions(box);
224	    const list = side === 'input' ? inputs : outputs;
225	    const p = list.find(p => p.name === port_name);
226	    return p ? { x: p.x, y: p.y } : null;
227	  }
228	  // }}}
229	
230	  return {
231	    boxes, BOX_W, BOX_HEADER, PORT_R,
232	    box_height, port_positions,
233	    draw_all, draw_box, hit_test_port, hit_test_box, get_port_world_pos,
234	  };
235	})();
236	
```

1	// Wire rendering, hit testing, and connection management.
2	// Wires are derived from box connection data — no separate wire store.
3	
4	const Wires = (() => {
5	  const BEZIER_CTRL_OFFSET = 80;
6	  // LOOP_OFFSET pulls a self-loop's control points further out and
7	  // below the box body, so the curve sweeps around the bottom rather
8	  // than cutting straight through the rectangle (issue 228).
9	  const LOOP_OFFSET        = 80;
10	  const HIT_SAMPLES        = 20;
11	  const HIT_THRESHOLD_PX   = 8;
12	
13	  // Colors for comparator branch wires; plain wire uses default
14	  const BRANCH_COLOR = { lt: '#ff8c42', eq: '#4a9eff', gt: '#4caf7d' };
15	
16	  let _selected_wire = null;  // { from_box, from_branch, to_box, to_input }
17	
18	  // {{{ selected_wire
19	  function selected_wire() { return _selected_wire; }
20	  // }}}
21	
22	  // {{{ bezier_point
23	  function bezier_point(t, x0, y0, cx0, cy0, cx1, cy1, x1, y1) {
24	    const mt = 1 - t;
25	    return {
26	      x: mt*mt*mt*x0 + 3*mt*mt*t*cx0 + 3*mt*t*t*cx1 + t*t*t*x1,
27	      y: mt*mt*mt*y0 + 3*mt*mt*t*cy0 + 3*mt*t*t*cy1 + t*t*t*y1,
28	    };
29	  }
30	  // }}}
31	
32	  // {{{ endpoint_controls
33	  // Returns the two cubic-Bezier control points for a wire from src to
34	  // dst. For a normal wire the controls jut horizontally out of each
35	  // port. For a self-loop (output and input on the same box body), the
36	  // controls swing out and below so the curve routes around the
37	  // rectangle instead of slicing through it (issue 228).
38	  function endpoint_controls(src, dst, self_loop_box) {
39	    if (self_loop_box) {
40	      const h = Boxes.box_height(self_loop_box);
41	      return {
42	        cx0: src.x + LOOP_OFFSET, cy0: src.y + h * 0.7,
43	        cx1: dst.x - LOOP_OFFSET, cy1: dst.y + h * 0.7,
44	      };
45	    }
46	    return {
47	      cx0: src.x + BEZIER_CTRL_OFFSET, cy0: src.y,
48	      cx1: dst.x - BEZIER_CTRL_OFFSET, cy1: dst.y,
49	    };
50	  }
51	  // }}}
52	
53	  // {{{ draw_bezier_cp
54	  // Draws a cubic Bezier with explicit control points. The wrapper
55	  // draw_bezier (kept for the in-progress wire-drag preview) calls
56	  // this with the default straight-out controls.
57	  function draw_bezier_cp(ctx, x0, y0, cx0, cy0, cx1, cy1, x1, y1, color, selected) {
58	    ctx.beginPath();
59	    ctx.moveTo(x0, y0);
60	    ctx.bezierCurveTo(cx0, cy0, cx1, cy1, x1, y1);
61	    ctx.strokeStyle = selected ? '#ffffff' : color;
62	    ctx.lineWidth   = selected ? 2.5 : 1.5;
63	    ctx.setLineDash([]);
64	    ctx.stroke();
65	  }
66	  // }}}
67	
68	  // {{{ draw_bezier
69	  function draw_bezier(ctx, x0, y0, x1, y1, color, selected) {
70	    const cx0 = x0 + BEZIER_CTRL_OFFSET;
71	    const cy0 = y0;
72	    const cx1 = x1 - BEZIER_CTRL_OFFSET;
73	    const cy1 = y1;
74	    draw_bezier_cp(ctx, x0, y0, cx0, cy0, cx1, cy1, x1, y1, color, selected);
75	  }
76	  // }}}
77	
78	  // {{{ hit_test_wire
79	  // Returns the connection record if cursor (wx,wy) is within HIT_THRESHOLD pixels
80	  // of any wire, or null. Operates in world space; threshold is adjusted for zoom.
81	  function hit_test_wire(wx, wy, zoom) {
82	    const threshold = HIT_THRESHOLD_PX / zoom;
83	    for (const id in Boxes.boxes) {
84	      const box = Boxes.boxes[id];
85	      for (const c of box.connections || []) {
86	        if (c.from_box !== id) continue;
87	
88	        const src  = Boxes.get_port_world_pos(c.from_box, c.from_branch ?? null, 'output');
89	        const dst  = Boxes.get_port_world_pos(c.to_box,   c.to_input,            'input');
90	        if (!src || !dst) continue;
91	
92	        // Self-loop wires use the looped control points, so sample
93	        // along that same curve — clicks register on the visual wire
94	        // path rather than on a phantom straight-through path.
95	        const self_box = (c.from_box === c.to_box) ? Boxes.boxes[c.from_box] : null;
96	        const { cx0, cy0, cx1, cy1 } = endpoint_controls(src, dst, self_box);
97	
98	        for (let i = 0; i <= HIT_SAMPLES; i++) {
99	          const t = i / HIT_SAMPLES;
100	          const p = bezier_point(t, src.x, src.y, cx0, cy0, cx1, cy1, dst.x, dst.y);
101	          const dx = wx - p.x, dy = wy - p.y;
102	          if (dx*dx + dy*dy <= threshold*threshold) return c;
103	        }
104	      }
105	    }
106	    return null;
107	  }
108	  // }}}
109	
110	  // {{{ draw_all
111	  function draw_all(ctx, selected_box_id) {
112	    for (const id in Boxes.boxes) {
113	      const box = Boxes.boxes[id];
114	      for (const c of box.connections || []) {
115	        if (c.from_box !== id) continue;
116	
117	        const src = Boxes.get_port_world_pos(c.from_box, c.from_branch ?? null, 'output');
118	        const dst = Boxes.get_port_world_pos(c.to_box,   c.to_input,            'input');
119	        if (!src || !dst) continue;
120	
121	        // comparator branches get distinct colors; plain wire uses dim default
122	        const color = c.from_branch ? (BRANCH_COLOR[c.from_branch] || '#4a6080') : '#4a6080';
123	
124	        const is_selected = _selected_wire &&
125	          _selected_wire.from_box    === c.from_box &&
126	          _selected_wire.from_branch === c.from_branch &&
127	          _selected_wire.to_box      === c.to_box &&
128	          _selected_wire.to_input    === c.to_input;
129	
130	        // self-loop wires need control points that route around the
131	        // box body; everything else uses the default straight-out controls
132	        const self_box = (c.from_box === c.to_box) ? box : null;
133	        const { cx0, cy0, cx1, cy1 } = endpoint_controls(src, dst, self_box);
134	        draw_bezier_cp(ctx, src.x, src.y, cx0, cy0, cx1, cy1, dst.x, dst.y,
135	                       color, is_selected);
136	      }
137	    }
138	  }
139	  // }}}
140	
141	  // Handle wire selection via click
142	  Canvas.el.addEventListener('click', e => {
143	    if (e.button !== 0) return;
144	    const s = Canvas.mouse_pos(e);
145	    const w = Canvas.screen_to_world(s.x, s.y);
146	
147	    // only try wire hit if no box was hit
148	    if (Boxes.hit_test_box(w.x, w.y)) { _selected_wire = null; return; }
149	    if (Boxes.hit_test_port(w.x, w.y)) return;
150	
151	    const wire = hit_test_wire(w.x, w.y, Canvas.cam.zoom);
152	    if (wire) {
153	      _selected_wire = wire;
154	      const branch_str = wire.from_branch ? '.' + wire.from_branch : '';
155	      status_msg('wire ' + wire.from_box + branch_str + ' → ' + wire.to_box + '.' +
156	        wire.to_input + ' — press Delete to remove');
157	    } else {
158	      _selected_wire = null;
159	    }
160	    Canvas.mark_dirty();
161	  });
162	
163	  // {{{ create_connection
164	  // from_branch: null (no comparator) or 'lt'/'eq'/'gt'.
165	  //
166	  // Mutates the cached box objects in place. Replacing the reference
167	  // (e.g. with a freshly-fetched copy from API.get_box) used to break
168	  // any other module holding a reference to the box — most painfully
169	  // the inspector, whose current_box reference would silently go
170	  // stale after a wire was created, causing subsequent variadic
171	  // operations to operate on out-of-date data and clobber the just-
172	  // added wire on save. Mutating in place keeps every reference
173	  // valid.
174	  async function create_connection(from_box_id, from_branch, to_box_id, to_input) {
175	    const src_box = Boxes.boxes[from_box_id];
176	    const dst_box = Boxes.boxes[to_box_id];
177	    if (!src_box || !dst_box) {
178	      status_msg('connect error: box not in cache', 'error');
179	      return;
180	    }
181	    // Defensive: a sink box has no output port (issue 226), so the
182	    // canvas wouldn't hand us a wire start from it. Refuse anyway in
183	    // case some other path tries to fabricate one.
184	    if (src_box.has_output === false) {
185	      status_msg('connect error: source box is a sink', 'error');
186	      return;
187	    }
188	
189	    const conn = {
190	      from_box:    from_box_id,
191	      from_branch: from_branch ?? null,
192	      to_box:      to_box_id,
193	      to_input:    to_input,
194	    };
195	
196	    src_box.connections = src_box.connections || [];
197	    dst_box.connections = dst_box.connections || [];
198	
199	    // avoid exact duplicates (same source, branch, dest, input)
200	    const dup = src_box.connections.some(c =>
201	      c.from_box    === conn.from_box    &&
202	      (c.from_branch ?? null) === conn.from_branch &&
203	      c.to_box      === conn.to_box      &&
204	      c.to_input    === conn.to_input
205	    );
206	    if (dup) return;
207	
208	    // For self-loops src_box === dst_box, so push only once; otherwise
209	    // both ends record the connection in their respective arrays
210	    // (issue 228).
211	    const self_loop = src_box === dst_box;
212	    src_box.connections.push(conn);
213	    if (!self_loop) dst_box.connections.push(conn);
214	
215	    try {
216	      if (self_loop) {
217	        await API.put_box(from_box_id, src_box);
218	      } else {
219	        await Promise.all([
220	          API.put_box(from_box_id, src_box),
221	          API.put_box(to_box_id,   dst_box),
222	        ]);
223	      }
224	    } catch (e) {
225	      // roll back the push so the in-memory state matches the server
226	      src_box.connections.pop();
227	      if (!self_loop) dst_box.connections.pop();
228	      status_msg('connect error: ' + e.message, 'error');
229	      return;
230	    }
231	
232	    const branch_str = from_branch ? '.' + from_branch : '';
233	    status_msg('connected ' + from_box_id + branch_str + ' → ' + to_box_id + '.' + to_input);
234	    Canvas.mark_dirty();
235	
236	    // Auto-grow the destination box's variadic group when the wire
237	    // landed on its last slot (issue 217 part B). Idempotent — the
238	    // helper checks first whether to_input is the last slot of its
239	    // group and exits if not.
240	    if (Inspector.auto_grow_after_set) {
241	      await Inspector.auto_grow_after_set(dst_box, to_input);
242	    }
243	    // Same idea on the source side: an iterator box (issue 221) auto-
244	    // appends a new placeholder slot when the user wires from its last
245	    // slot. The helper is a no-op if from_branch isn't the last slot
246	    // (or the box isn't an iterator).
247	    if (Inspector.auto_grow_iterator_after_connect && from_branch) {
248	      await Inspector.auto_grow_iterator_after_connect(src_box, from_branch);
249	    }
250	  }
251	  // }}}
252	
253	  // {{{ delete_connection
254	  // Same in-place mutation pattern as create_connection — never
255	  // replace the cached references.
256	  async function delete_connection(conn) {
257	    const src_box = Boxes.boxes[conn.from_box];
258	    const dst_box = Boxes.boxes[conn.to_box];
259	    if (!src_box || !dst_box) {
260	      status_msg('delete wire error: box not in cache', 'error');
261	      return;
262	    }
263	
264	    const match = c =>
265	      c.from_box    === conn.from_box    &&
266	      (c.from_branch ?? null) === (conn.from_branch ?? null) &&
267	      c.to_box      === conn.to_box      &&
268	      c.to_input    === conn.to_input;
269	
270	    // Self-loops: src_box === dst_box, so a single filter+PUT covers
271	    // both ends of the conceptual connection (issue 228).
272	    const self_loop   = src_box === dst_box;
273	    const src_before  = src_box.connections || [];
274	    const dst_before  = dst_box.connections || [];
275	    src_box.connections = src_before.filter(c => !match(c));
276	    if (!self_loop) dst_box.connections = dst_before.filter(c => !match(c));
277	
278	    try {
279	      if (self_loop) {
280	        await API.put_box(conn.from_box, src_box);
281	      } else {
282	        await Promise.all([
283	          API.put_box(conn.from_box, src_box),
284	          API.put_box(conn.to_box,   dst_box),
285	        ]);
286	      }
287	    } catch (e) {
288	      // roll back
289	      src_box.connections = src_before;
290	      if (!self_loop) dst_box.connections = dst_before;
291	      status_msg('delete wire error: ' + e.message, 'error');
292	      return;
293	    }
294	
295	    _selected_wire = null;
296	    status_msg('deleted connection');
297	    Canvas.mark_dirty();
298	  }
299	  // }}}
300	
301	  return { draw_all, draw_bezier, hit_test_wire, create_connection,
302	           delete_connection, selected_wire };
303	})();
304	
```

12	
13	  // Issue 235: input ports with a literal value show the value on the

18

28	  // Issue 235 surfaces this by deciding the canvas label — port name
29	  // when wired (so the reader doesn't mistake the dormant value for
30	  // what's flowing through), value otherwise.
31	  function port_has_incoming_wire(box, port_name) {
32	    if (!box.connections) return false;
33	    for (const c of box.connections) {
34	      if (c.to_box === box.id && c.to_input === port_name) return true;
35	    }
36	    return false;
37	  }
38	  // }}}
39	
40	  // {{{ port_label_text
41	  // Picks the string drawn next to an input port dot. Three cases:
42	  //   - wire attached, or no value → port name (parameter identity)
43	  //   - value set, no wire         → the value (what the box does)
44	  //   - value too long / multiline → first line, truncated with …
45	  // The two-branch decision is the visible heart of issue 235: a
46	  // reader scanning the map should see the data the box operates on,
47	  // not the parameter slot it sits in.
48	  function port_label_text(box, i) {
49	    const p = box.inputs[i];
50	    const has_value = p.value !== undefined && p.value !== null && p.value !== '';
51	    if (!has_value)                              return p.name;
52	    if (port_has_incoming_wire(box, p.name))     return p.name;
53	    const raw        = String(p.value);

55	    const multiline  = first_line.length < raw.length;
56	    if (first_line.length > MAX_LABEL_CHARS) {

58	    }

67	    // Output rows: iterator boxes have N named slots (issue 221);
68	    // comparator active: 3 dots (lt/eq/gt); sink: 0; otherwise 1.
69	    let n_out;

79	    return Math.max(MIN_BOX_H, BOX_HEADER + Math.max(n_in, n_out) * PORT_ROW_H + 8);

1	// Canvas viewport: pan + zoom, and the per-frame draw transform.
2	//
3	// Coordinate model
4	//   screen pixel (sx, sy)  ←→  world point (wx, wy)
5	//     sx = wx * zoom + pan_x
6	//     wx = (sx - pan_x) / zoom
7	//
8	// `pan_x` / `pan_y` are screen-pixel offsets passed to ctx.translate
9	// AFTER scaling: the world is scaled around its origin first, then the
10	// result is shifted on screen. This is simpler than a world-space camera
11	// because every operation is either pure-screen-pixel arithmetic
12	// (panning, mouse positions) or pure-world arithmetic (drawing). They
13	// only meet in two functions: world_to_screen and screen_to_world.
14	
15	const Canvas = (() => {
16	  const el  = document.getElementById('canvas');
17	  const ctx = el.getContext('2d');
18	
19	  let zoom  = 1;
20	  let pan_x = 0;
21	  let pan_y = 0;
22	
23	  let dirty = true;
24	
25	  // {{{ resize
26	  function resize() {
27	    el.width  = window.innerWidth  - (document.getElementById('sidebar').offsetWidth || 0);
28	    el.height = window.innerHeight;
29	    dirty = true;
30	  }
31	  // }}}
32	
33	  // {{{ world_to_screen
34	  function world_to_screen(wx, wy) {
35	    return { x: wx * zoom + pan_x, y: wy * zoom + pan_y };
36	  }
37	  // }}}
38	
39	  // {{{ screen_to_world
40	  function screen_to_world(sx, sy) {
41	    return { x: (sx - pan_x) / zoom, y: (sy - pan_y) / zoom };
42	  }
43	  // }}}
44	
45	  // {{{ mark_dirty
46	  function mark_dirty() { dirty = true; }
47	  // }}}
48	
49	  // {{{ start_frame
50	  // Reset the transform, clear, then set up the world-drawing transform.

21	  function show_ctx_menu(screen_x, screen_y, items) {
22	    ctx_menu.innerHTML = '';
23	    items.forEach(item => {
24	      if (item === 'sep') {
25	        const el = document.createElement('div');
26	        el.className = 'ctx-sep';
27	        ctx_menu.appendChild(el);
28	        return;
29	      }
30	      const el = document.createElement('div');
31	      el.className = 'ctx-item' + (item.danger ? ' danger' : '');
32	      el.textContent = item.label;
33	      el.onclick = () => { hide_ctx_menu(); item.action(); };
34	      ctx_menu.appendChild(el);
35	    });
36	    ctx_menu.style.display = 'block';
37	    ctx_menu.style.left = screen_x + 'px';
38	    ctx_menu.style.top  = screen_y + 'px';
39	    // keep on screen
40	    const r = ctx_menu.getBoundingClientRect();
```

235 implementation is in place. Server is running at `http://localhost:7711` for you to verify. Summary of what changed:

- **`assets/js/002-boxes.js`** — added `port_has_incoming_wire`, `port_label_text`, and `port_full_value_if_truncated` helpers; `draw_box` now picks the input label via `port_label_text` instead of always rendering the port name.
- **`assets/index.html`** — added `#canvas-tooltip` styled div positioned absolutely over the canvas.
- **`assets/js/005-app.js`** — `show_tooltip` / `hide_tooltip` helpers; the mousemove handler now hides the tooltip by default and reinstates it when the cursor is over an input port whose label is a truncated value; mouseleave hides too.

Unit-tested the pure label-picking logic against 8 cases (no value, value, wired-over-value, empty string, multi-line, long-truncated, numeric, escape-encoded separator) — all pass.

**What I cannot verify from here**: that it looks right in the browser — I'm headless, so I can't confirm the value renders cleanly in the canvas font, that the tooltip lands in the right spot, or that hovering off a port hides it. Could you open `http://localhost:7711` against `branch-test` (its `write-high` box has a `n=5` literal with a wire — should still show `n`), and against `classify-demo`'s router (literal `"SoraMech"` in a predicate, not on an input port — separate path, won't trigger this)? ### [253] type=assistant
ts: 2026-05-19T07:32:21.060Z

Quick clarifications first, then the location list and the commit.

**Tooltips**: they only appear when the canvas label had to be shortened — i.e. the value is longer than 24 chars or contains a real newline. Your test cases (`5`, etc.) were short enough to render in full, so no tooltip was warranted. If you set a port value to something like `"this is a very long literal that won't fit in the box"` and hover the port dot, you should see the full text float near the cursor.

**"Hovering on a port should hide it?"**: my fault, that was a confusingly worded sentence. I meant *mouseleave on the whole canvas* (cursor exits the canvas area entirely) clears any showing tooltip — pure housekeeping so a stale tooltip doesn't linger. Hovering a port does NOT hide anything.

**Editable title overrides filename**: yes, 236 already specs exactly that — `box.label` overrides the filename default. Good, no change needed.

Now the script-location question.

### [306] type=assistant
ts: 2026-05-19T07:53:39.627Z

Committed (commit `2ce3f72`). Only my 10 files; the other dev's working-tree changes are untouched.

Now the script-location question — a canonical map of every place runnable / loadable code lives in this project:

| Location | What it holds | Who runs it |
|---|---|---|
| `scripts/` | Top-level operator scripts: `start-server.sh`, `run-tests.sh`, `create-map.sh`, `open-editor.sh`, `soramech-compile.sh`, `test-server.sh` | You, from a shell |
| `demo.sh`, `run` (root) | Phase-demo entry points | You, from a shell |
| `src/` | Editor server + executor + schema/loader in Lua (`001-005-*.lua`), plus phase-3 C runtime (`008-014-*.c`) | The server / pool runtime |
| `assets/js/` | Editor frontend modules (`001-canvas.js` through `008-source-view.js`) | The browser |
| `drivers/` | Per-language driver shims (`bash.sh`, `c.sh`, `lua.sh`) that bridge a box invocation to its language | The executor |
| `langs/<lang>/` | Phase-3 language-spec C glue and per-language build (`spec.c`, `Makefile`, e.g. `langs/bash/bash-server.sh`) | The dispatch layer |
| `libs/` | Bundled "library box" modules every map can `ref` directly: `text.lua`, `files.lua`, `ollama.lua`, `soramech-data.lua`, `dkjson.lua`, plus the C sub-libs `json/` and `task-pool/` | Boxes via `ref` |
| `maps/<map>/src/` | Per-map user scripts — the actual functions called by each `call` box in that map | The executor when running that map |
| `tests/maps/<map>/src/` | Same idea but inside the test-map fixtures | The C test binaries |
| `tests/` | C test binaries and test runners | `scripts/run-tests.sh` |

If you're looking for something specific and can't find it, the first two places to check are `libs/` (bundled, project-wide) and `maps/<your-map>/src/` (per-map). Everything else is infrastructure.

Ready to start on 236 — say go when you've decided.

### [310] type=assistant

### [311] type=assistant
ts: 2026-05-19T07:56:28.590Z

Two places, by scope:

- **`libs/`** — bundled, shared across every map. Currently: `text.lua`, `files.lua`, `ollama.lua`, `soramech-data.lua`, `dkjson.lua`. Any map's call box can `ref` these directly.
- **`maps/<map>/src/`** — scoped to one map. Scripts only that map's boxes can ref.

That's the whole toolbox surface. Issue 238 (the editor-server config file) is the one that would let a user add their own bundled-default directory alongside `libs/`, so they could keep their personal toolbox somewhere outside this repo and still have it show up in the file browser.

1	#!/usr/bin/env bash
2	# Creates a new SoraMech map directory with all required files and a tmp/ symlink.
3	# The map directory is created under ${DIR}/maps/<name>.
4	# Run: scripts/create-map.sh <map-name> [maps-root]
5	
6	DIR="/mnt/mtwo/programs/sora/soramech"
7	
8	MAP_NAME="${1}"
9	MAPS_ROOT="${2:-${DIR}/maps}"
10	
11	if [ -z "${MAP_NAME}" ]; then
12	    echo "usage: create-map.sh <map-name> [maps-root]" >&2
13	    exit 1
14	fi
15	
16	MAP_DIR="${MAPS_ROOT}/${MAP_NAME}"
17	
18	if [ -d "${MAP_DIR}" ]; then
19	    echo "error: map directory already exists: ${MAP_DIR}" >&2
20	    exit 1
21	fi
22	
23	# create directory structure
24	mkdir -p "${MAP_DIR}/boxes"
25	mkdir -p "${MAP_DIR}/data"
26	mkdir -p "${MAP_DIR}/src"
27	mkdir -p "${MAP_DIR}/drivers"
28	
29	# create /tmp target before symlinking — logs/ and cache/ live there at runtime
30	TMP_TARGET="/tmp/soramech-${MAP_NAME}"
31	mkdir -p "${TMP_TARGET}"
32	
33	# tmp/ is a symlink to /tmp/soramech-<name>/ so ephemeral files stay in RAM
34	ln -s "${TMP_TARGET}" "${MAP_DIR}/tmp"
35	
36	# write meta.json — src_dirs seeds the file browser with this map's own src/ dir
37	cat > "${MAP_DIR}/meta.json" << METAJSON
38	{
39	  "name": "${MAP_NAME}",
40	  "description": "",
41	  "entry_box_id": "",
42	  "src_dirs": ["${MAP_DIR}/src"]
43	}
44	METAJSON
45	
46	# write drivers.json pointing at the built-in driver scripts in the soramech root
47	cat > "${MAP_DIR}/drivers.json" << DRIVERSJSON
48	{
49	  ".lua": "${DIR}/drivers/lua.sh",
50	  ".c":   "${DIR}/drivers/c.sh",
51	  ".sh":  "${DIR}/drivers/bash.sh"
52	}
53	DRIVERSJSON
54	
55	echo "created: ${MAP_DIR}"
56	echo "next: edit meta.json to set entry_box_id, then add boxes in ${MAP_DIR}/boxes/"
57

### [331] type=assistant
ts: 2026-05-19T08:05:10.149Z

No, and that's worth flagging — `libs/` is **not** pre-selected by default. Here's what `scripts/create-map.sh` actually does today:

A new map's `meta.json` seeds `src_dirs` with just **the map's own `src/` directory**. The file browser only lists what's in `src_dirs`. So `libs/` won't appear in a freshly-created map until the user adds it via the file-browser's dir-picker (or edits `meta.json` by hand).

There's a partial-truth-counterpart though: at runtime, the server's `package.path` includes `${DIR}/libs/?.lua`, so a function that does `require("text")` resolves to `libs/text.lua` whether or not the editor knows about it. That means `libs/` is **available for Lua to load**, just **not browseable in the editor by default**.

That gap is exactly what issue 238 is set up to fix — the config file would carry a `bundled_script_dirs` list (defaulting to `[libs/]`) that `create-map.sh` (or its replacement) drops straight into every new map's `src_dirs`. After 238 lands, "every new map sees `libs/` in the file browser without the user lifting a finger" would be the default.

Want me to:

- **(a)** add a small note to 238 making the create-map auto-population explicit (it's implied but not spelled out), or
- **(b)** open it as a separate tiny issue ("create-map.sh seeds bundled dirs from server config") so the two changes can land independently, or
- **(c)** leave 238 as-is and treat the auto-population as obvious-on-implementation?

644

646	        local client, cerr = server:accept()
647	        if client then
648	            client:settimeout(5)
649	            local t_start = socket.gettime()

1	// Source file browser and function signature parser.
2	// Renders into the inspector panel: file list → function list → auto-populate box ports.

4	const FileBrowser = (() => {
5	
6	  // {{{ lua_fn_end
7	  // Returns the index of the closing 'end'/'until' that matches the function body
8	  // starting at body_start. Tracks nesting depth by counting block openers and closers.
9	  // Does not attempt to skip string literals — good enough for well-formatted source.
10	  function lua_fn_end(content, body_start) {
11	    const tok = /\b(function|if|while|for|repeat|do|end|until)\b/g;
12	    tok.lastIndex = body_start;
13	    let depth = 1;
14	    let m;
15	    while ((m = tok.exec(content)) !== null) {
16	      const kw = m[1];
17	      if (kw === 'end' || kw === 'until') {
18	        depth--;
19	        if (depth === 0) return m.index;
20	      } else {
21	        depth++;
22	      }
23	    }
24	    return content.length;
25	  }
26	  // }}}
27	
28	  // {{{ parse_lua_returns
29	  // Scans a function body for return statements; returns an array of output names.
30	  function parse_lua_returns(body) {
31	    const re    = /\breturn\b\s*([^\n]+)/g;
32	    const names = new Set();
33	    let m;
34	    while ((m = re.exec(body)) !== null) {
35	      const expr = m[1].replace(/--.*$/, '').trim();
36	      if (!expr) continue;
37	      // string literals or concat operator mean the whole expression is complex
38	      if (/["']|\.\./.test(expr)) { names.add('result'); continue; }
39	      const parts = expr.split(',');
40	      parts.forEach(part => {
41	        part = part.trim();
42	        // bare identifier → use as port name
43	        if (/^[a-zA-Z_]\w*$/.test(part) &&
44	            !['nil','true','false','not','and','or','end'].includes(part)) {
45	          names.add(part);
46	        } else if (parts.length === 1 && part) {
47	          // single complex expression → call it "result"
48	          names.add('result');
49	        }
50	        // multi-value complex expression with no bare names: skip
51	      });
52	    }
53	    return [...names];
54	  }
55	  // }}}
56	
57	  // {{{ parse_lua
58	  function parse_lua(content) {
59	    const fns    = [];
60	    const fn_re  = /^function\s+M\.(\w+)\s*\(([^)]*)\)/mg;
61	    let m;
62	    while ((m = fn_re.exec(content)) !== null) {
63	      const body_start = m.index + m[0].length;
64	      const body_end   = lua_fn_end(content, body_start);
65	      const body       = content.slice(body_start, body_end);
66	      const inputs     = m[2].trim()
67	        ? m[2].split(',').map(s => s.trim()).filter(Boolean)
68	        : [];
69	      const outputs    = parse_lua_returns(body);
70	      fns.push({ name: m[1], inputs, outputs });
71	    }
72	    return fns;
73	  }
74	  // }}}
75	
76	  // {{{ parse_bash_inputs
77	  function parse_bash_inputs(body) {
78	    const params = {};
79	    // local var="${N}" or local var="$N"
80	    const local_re = /local\s+(\w+)\s*=\s*["']?\$\{?(\d+)\}?["']?/g;
81	    let m;
82	    while ((m = local_re.exec(body)) !== null) {
83	      params[parseInt(m[2])] = m[1];
84	    }
85	    if (Object.keys(params).length > 0) {
86	      const max = Math.max(...Object.keys(params).map(Number));
87	      return Array.from({ length: max }, (_, i) => params[i + 1] || `arg${i + 1}`);
88	    }
89	    // fall back: scan for raw $N references
90	    const seen = new Set();
91	    const pos_re = /\$\{?(\d+)\}?/g;
92	    while ((m = pos_re.exec(body)) !== null) {
93	      const n = parseInt(m[1]);
94	      if (n > 0) seen.add(n);
95	    }
96	    if (seen.size > 0) {
97	      const max = Math.max(...seen);
98	      return Array.from({ length: max }, (_, i) => `arg${i + 1}`);
99	    }
100	    return [];
101	  }
102	  // }}}
103	
104	  // {{{ parse_bash
105	  function parse_bash(content) {
106	    const fns   = [];
107	    const re    = /^(\w+)\s*\(\s*\)\s*\{/mg;
108	    const skip  = new Set(['if','while','for','case','do','done','fi','then','else']);
109	    let m;
110	    while ((m = re.exec(content)) !== null) {
111	      const name = m[1];
112	      if (skip.has(name)) continue;
113	      const body_start = m.index + m[0].length;
114	      const close      = content.indexOf('\n}', body_start);
115	      const body       = content.slice(body_start, close > -1 ? close : body_start + 800);
116	      const inputs     = parse_bash_inputs(body);
117	      fns.push({ name, inputs, outputs: ['output'] });

119	    return fns;
120	  }

284	    if (!menu) {
285	      menu = document.createElement('div');
286	      menu.id = 'fb-ctx-menu';
287	      menu.style.cssText =
288	        'position:fixed;background:#181c28;border:1px solid #2a2f45;' +
289	        'border-radius:4px;padding:4px 0;z-index:1001;min-width:150px;' +
290	        'box-shadow:0 4px 16px #00000066;display:none;';
291	      document.body.appendChild(menu);
292	      // close on any click or Escape — attached once at creation
293	      document.addEventListener('click', () => { menu.style.display = 'none'; });
294	      document.addEventListener('keydown', e => {
295	        if (e.key === 'Escape') menu.style.display = 'none';
296	      });
297	    }
298	    menu.innerHTML = '';
299	    items.forEach(item => {
300	      const el = document.createElement('div');
301	      el.className  = item.danger ? 'ctx-item danger' : 'ctx-item';
302	      el.textContent = item.label;
303	      // stopPropagation so the document click listener above doesn't immediately close it
304	      el.addEventListener('click', e => { e.stopPropagation(); menu.style.display = 'none'; item.action(); });
305	      menu.appendChild(el);
306	    });
307	    menu.style.left    = screen_x + 'px';
308	    menu.style.top     = screen_y + 'px';
309	    menu.style.display = 'block';
310	    const r = menu.getBoundingClientRect();
311	    if (r.right  > window.innerWidth)  menu.style.left = (screen_x - r.width)  + 'px';
312	    if (r.bottom > window.innerHeight) menu.style.top  = (screen_y - r.height) + 'px';

335	    // build group list — every entry has a path; no exceptions
336	    const groups = [];
337	    (src_dirs || []).forEach(d => {
338	      if (d.files && d.files.length > 0) {
339	        groups.push({
340	          label:      d.label + '/',
341	          path:       d.path,
342	          files:      d.files,
343	          fetch_file: f => API.get_extra_src_file(d.index, f),
344	        });
345	      }
346	    });
347	
348	    const show_file_list = () => {
349	      container.innerHTML = '';
350	
351	      // add-library-dir button
352	      const add_btn = document.createElement('button');
353	      add_btn.textContent = '+ library dir';
354	      add_btn.className   = 'fb-add-dir-btn';
355	      add_btn.onclick = () => {
356	        // open picker at parent of last known dir so the user lands near recent work
357	        const last = groups.length > 0 ? groups[groups.length - 1].path : null;
358	        const initial = last ? (last.replace(/\/[^/]+$/, '') || '/') : '/home';
359	        show_dir_picker(container,
360	          async (path) => {
361	            try {
362	              const meta = await API.get_meta();
363	              // derive current list from groups, not meta.src_dirs:
364	              // groups already reflects the server-migrated state (extra_src_dirs + src/),
365	              // whereas meta.src_dirs is absent on old maps and would discard prior entries
366	              const current = groups.map(g => g.path);
367	              if (!current.includes(path)) {
368	                current.push(path);
369	                await API.put_meta({ ...meta, src_dirs: current });
370	              }
371	              render(container, on_select);
372	            } catch(e) {
373	              show_file_list();
374	              const note = document.createElement('div');
375	              note.className   = 'fb-note error';
376	              note.textContent = 'could not save dir: ' + e.message;
377	              container.insertBefore(note, container.firstChild);
378	            }
379	          },
380	          () => show_file_list(),
381	          initial
382	        );
383	      };
384	      container.appendChild(add_btn);
385	
386	      if (groups.length === 0) {
387	        const note = document.createElement('div');
388	        note.className   = 'fb-note';
389	        note.textContent = 'src/ is empty — add source files or a library dir';
390	        container.appendChild(note);
391	        return;
392	      }
393	
394	      groups.forEach(group => {
395	        // use full path as the collapse key for extra dirs so same-named dirs don't share state
396	        const collapse_key = group.path || group.label;
397	        const is_collapsed = collapsed.has(collapse_key);
398	
399	        // group header row with toggle
400	        const hdr = document.createElement('div');
401	        hdr.className = 'fb-group-hdr';
402	
403	        const toggle = document.createElement('span');
404	        toggle.className   = 'fb-group-toggle';
405	        toggle.textContent = is_collapsed ? '▶' : '▼';
406	        hdr.appendChild(toggle);
407	
408	        const lbl = document.createElement('span');
409	        lbl.className   = 'fb-header';
410	        lbl.textContent = group.label;
411	        hdr.appendChild(lbl);
412	
413	        hdr.onclick = () => {
414	          if (collapsed.has(collapse_key)) collapsed.delete(collapse_key);
415	          else collapsed.add(collapse_key);
416	          show_file_list();
417	        };
418	
419	        // right-click on any dir header: offer "hide directory"
420	        hdr.addEventListener('contextmenu', e => {
421	          e.preventDefault();
422	          show_fb_ctx_menu(e.clientX, e.clientY, [{
423	            label:  'hide directory',
424	            danger: true,
425	            action: async () => {
426	              try {
427	                const meta = await API.get_meta();
428	                // same migration rationale as add: use groups as source of truth
429	                const dirs = groups.map(g => g.path).filter(p => p !== group.path);
430	                await API.put_meta({ ...meta, src_dirs: dirs });
431	                render(container, on_select);
432	              } catch (err) {
433	                show_file_list();
434	                const note = document.createElement('div');
435	                note.className   = 'fb-note error';
436	                note.textContent = 'could not hide dir: ' + err.message;
437	                container.insertBefore(note, container.firstChild);
438	              }
439	            },
440	          }]);
441	        });
442	
443	        container.appendChild(hdr);
444	
445	        if (is_collapsed) return;
446	
447	        group.files.forEach(filename => {
448	          const row = document.createElement('div');
449	          row.className   = 'fb-file-row';
450	          // Prefix matches the dir picker's `·` for files so the same
451	          // glyph means the same thing across both views (issue 225).
452	          row.textContent = '· ' + filename;
453	          row.onclick     = async () => {
454	            row.textContent = filename + ' …';
455	            let content;
456	            try {
457	              content = await group.fetch_file(filename);
458	            } catch (e) {
459	              container.innerHTML = '<div class="fb-note error">could not read ' + filename + ': ' + e.message + '</div>';
460	              show_file_list();
461	              return;
462	            }
463	            const fns = parse_functions(content, filename);
464	            render_fn_list(container, group.label + filename, fns,
465	              (f, fn) => on_select(f, fn),
466	              show_file_list
467	            );
468	          };
469	          container.appendChild(row);
470	        });
471	      });
472	    };
473	
474	    show_file_list();
475	  }
476	  // }}}
477	
478	  // {{{ render_at_fn_list
479	  // Public entry point used by the inspector's "fn" button (issue
480	  // 224 follow-up). Jumps straight to the function picker for the
481	  // file at `ref`, with a back button that unwinds to the full
482	  // browser. Useful when the user wants to pick a different function
483	  // from the same file without re-walking the directory tree.
484	  async function render_at_fn_list(container, ref, on_select) {
485	    if (!ref) {
486	      // No ref to land on — open the full browser instead.
487	      return render(container, on_select);
488	    }
489	
490	    container.innerHTML = '<div class="fb-note">loading…</div>';
491	    const stripped = ref.replace(/^src\//, '');
492	
493	    let content = null;
494	    let error_msg = null;
495	
496	    // Mirror fetch_source's resolution order: main src/ first, then
497	    // each extra dir in turn. The first successful fetch wins.
498	    try {
499	      content = await API.get_src_file(stripped);
500	    } catch (e) { /* try extras */ }
501	
502	    if (content === null) {
503	      let extras = [];
504	      try { extras = await API.list_extra_src(); } catch (e) {}
505	      for (let i = 0; i < (extras || []).length; i++) {
506	        try { content = await API.get_extra_src_file(i, stripped); break; }
507	        catch (e) {}
508	      }
509	    }
510	
511	    if (content === null) {
512	      container.innerHTML = '';
513	      const note = document.createElement('div');
514	      note.className   = 'fb-note error';
515	      note.textContent = 'source not found: ' + ref;
516	      container.appendChild(note);
517	      const back = document.createElement('button');
518	      back.className   = 'fb-back';
519	      back.textContent = '← browse';
520	      back.onclick     = () => render(container, on_select);
521	      container.appendChild(back);
522	      return;
523	    }
524	
525	    const fns = parse_functions(content, stripped);
526	    render_fn_list(container, stripped, fns,
527	      (f, fn) => on_select(f, fn),
528	      () => render(container, on_select));
529	  }
530	  // }}}
531	
532	  return { parse_functions, render, render_at_fn_list };
533	})();
534	
```

The honest answer is \"edit the config file.\"\n        if (group.kind !== 'bundled') {\n          hdr.addEventListener('contextmenu', e => {\n            e.preventDefault();\n            show_fb_ctx_menu(e.clientX, e.clientY, [{\n              label:  'hide directory',\n              danger: true,\n              action: async () => {\n                try {\n                  const meta = await API.get_meta();\n                  // Same migration rationale as add: use groups as source\n                  // of truth. Config file present → dofile it; any missing field falls back\n--      to the value embedded in DEFAULT_CONFIG_TEXT (parsed once at\n--      startup) so partial configs keep working.\n--   2. Config file absent → generate the default template at the\n--      target path, then load the freshly-written file. The\n--      hard-wired defaults are NOT a separate code path — they live\n--      in DEFAULT_CONFIG_TEXT and are read back from disk after\n--      generation, so the on-disk file is always the source of\n--      truth on the next boot.\n-- All paths are resolved here so downstream code sees absolute\n-- paths only.\nlocal function load_server_config(path, project_root)\n    if not file_exists(path) then\n        generate_default_config(path)\n    end\n\n    local ok, user = pcall(dofile, path)\n    if not ok or type(user) ~= \"table\" then\n        io.stderr:write(\"config file at \" .. path .. \" did not return a table: \"\n            .. tostring(user) .. \"\\n\")\n        os.exit(1)\n    end\n\n    -- Parse the embedded template separately so we can use it for\n    -- field-level fallbacks. dostring via load() — runs in-memory,\n    -- no second disk read.\n    local defaults_fn = load(DEFAULT_CONFIG_TEXT, \"default-config\", \"t\")\n    local defaults    = defaults_fn and defaults_fn() or {}\n    for k, v in pairs(defaults) do\n        if user[k] == nil then user[k] = v end\n    end\n\n    user.map_root = resolve_path(user.map_root, project_root)\n    local resolved_dirs = {}\n    for _, d in ipairs(user.bundled_script_dirs or {}) do\n        local r = resolve_path(d, project_root)\n        if r then resolved_dirs[#resolved_dirs + 1] = r end\n    end\n    user.bundled_script_dirs = resolved_dirs\n    return user\nend\n-- }}}",
  "file_path":"/mnt/mtwo/programs/sora/soramech/src/006-server-main.lua",
  "replace_all":false,
  "old_string":"-- {{{ load_server_config\n-- Pulls config/editor-server.lua if present; falls back to defaults\n-- when missing, malformed, or partial. The file is OPTIONAL — a user\n-- can run the server with no config at all and get sane behavior.\n-- All paths are resolved here so downstream code sees absolute paths\n-- only.\nlocal function load_server_config(path, project_root)\n    local defaults = {\n        port = 7700,\n        map_root = \"maps\",\n        bundled_script_dirs = { \"libs\" },\n    }\n    local user = {}\n    local ok, loaded = pcall(dofile, path)\n    if ok and type(loaded) == \"table\" then\n        user = loaded\n    end\n    -- Field-by-field merge keeps each missing entry independently\n    -- defaulted, so a config that only sets `port` still benefits\n    -- from the bundled-dirs default.\n    for k, v in pairs(defaults) do\n        if user[k] == nil then user[k] = v end\n    end\n    user.map_root = resolve_path(user.map_root, project_root)\n    local resolved_dirs = {}\n    for _, d in ipairs(user.bundled_script_dirs or {}) do\n        local r = resolve_path(d, project_root)\n        if r then resolved_dirs[#resolved_dirs + 1] = r end\n    end\n    user.bundled_script_dirs = resolved_dirs\n    return user\nend\n-- }}}"
}

{
  "new_string":"        // Every dir gets a \"bundled\" tag — they're all library\n        // directories from the map's point of view, whether the\n        // path lives in the editor config or in this map's meta.\n        // The user asked for a uniform treatment (issue 238) so the\n        // visual cue doesn't single one kind out as special.\n        const tag = document.createElement('span');\n        tag.textContent = ' bundled';\n        tag.style.cssText = 'font-size:9px;color:#4caf7d;margin-left:6px;' +\n          'border:1px solid #4caf7d;padding:0 4px;border-radius:2px;';\n        hdr.appendChild(tag);\n\n        hdr.onclick = () => {\n          if (collapsed.has(collapse_key)) collapsed.delete(collapse_key);\n          else collapsed.add(collapse_key);\n          show_file_list();\n        };\n\n        // Right-click → \"hide directory\" works on every group. The\n        // storage differs by kind: map dirs come out of src_dirs;\n        // editor-wide bundled dirs are recorded in\n        // hidden_bundled_dirs (an opt-out list the server checks\n        // when merging in the config defaults). The user\n        // experiences both as the same action.\n        hdr.addEventListener('contextmenu', e => {\n          e.preventDefault();\n          show_fb_ctx_menu(e.clientX, e.clientY, [{\n            label:  'hide directory',\n            danger: true,\n            action: async () => {\n              try {\n                const meta = await API.get_meta();\n                let patch;\n                if (group.kind === 'bundled') {\n                  // record the opt-out; the server filters this\n                  // path out of the bundled list on next load\n                  const hidden = Array.isArray(meta.hidden_bundled_dirs)\n                    ? meta.hidden_bundled_dirs.slice() : [];\n                  if (!hidden.includes(group.path)) hidden.push(group.path);\n                  patch = { ...meta, hidden_bundled_dirs: hidden };\n                } else {\n                  // drop from src_dirs; bundled paths never bleed\n                  // into the per-map list\n                  const dirs = groups\n                    .filter(g => g.kind !== 'bundled' && g.path !== group.path)\n                    .map(g => g.path);\n                  patch = { ...meta, src_dirs: dirs };\n                }\n                await API.put_meta(patch);\n                render(container, on_select);\n              } catch (err) {\n                show_file_list();\n                const note = document.createElement('div');\n                note.className   = 'fb-note error';\n                note.textContent = 'could not hide dir: ' + err.message;\n                container.insertBefore(note, container.firstChild);\n              }\n            },\n          }]);\n        });",
  "file_path":"/mnt/mtwo/programs/sora/soramech/assets/js/007-filebrowser.js",

  "old_string":"        // Bundled dirs (issue 238) get a small tag so the user can\n        // tell their own toolbox apart from the editor-wide defaults.\n        if (group.kind === 'bundled') {\n          const tag = document.createElement('span');\n          tag.textContent = ' bundled';\n          tag.style.cssText = 'font-size:9px;color:#4caf7d;margin-left:6px;' +\n            'border:1px solid #4caf7d;padding:0 4px;border-radius:2px;';\n          hdr.appendChild(tag);\n        }\n\n        hdr.onclick = () => {\n          if (collapsed.has(collapse_key)) collapsed.delete(collapse_key);\n          else collapsed.add(collapse_key);\n          show_file_list();\n        };\n\n        // Right-click on a map-owned dir header offers \"hide\n        // directory\" — removes it from meta.json. Bundled dirs skip\n        // this menu entirely: they aren't in meta.json (they come\n        // from the server's config file), so removing them from\n        // meta.json would be a no-op the user would then have to\n        // figure out. The honest answer is \"edit the config file.\"\n        if (group.kind !== 'bundled') {\n          hdr.addEventListener('contextmenu', e => {\n            e.preventDefault();\n            show_fb_ctx_menu(e.clientX, e.clientY, [{\n              label:  'hide directory',\n              danger: true,\n              action: async () => {\n                try {\n                  const meta = await API.get_meta();\n                  // Same migration rationale as add: use groups as source\n                  // of truth. Yes — user-added dirs (the ones you'd add via the `+ library dir` button) are treated identically. They live in `meta.src_dirs` alongside the map's own `src/`, so on the server they're tagged `kind=map`, and in the file browser they get the same green `bundled` tag and the same right-click → hide menu. Hiding one of them just removes its entry from `meta.src_dirs`.

So the full picture in the browser right now is:

| dir origin | where it's stored | tag | hide action |
|---|---|---|---|
| map's own `src/` | `meta.src_dirs` (or implicit fallback) | bundled | drop from `src_dirs` |
| `+ library dir` (per-map add) | `meta.src_dirs` | bundled | drop from `src_dirs` |
| editor config `bundled_script_dirs` | `config/editor-server.lua` | bundled | add to `meta.hidden_bundled_dirs` |

Three storage tiers, one UI surface. Want me to confirm in the browser then close it out?

{
  "new_string":"# 238 — Editor HTTP server config file (default bundled-script locations)\n\n## Status\ncomplete\n\n## Implementation notes\n\n`config/editor-server.lua` is the user-facing knob — a single Lua\ntable returning `port`, `map_root`, and `bundled_script_dirs`. The\nloader in `src/006-server-main.lua` does three things:\n\n- If the file is missing, **generates it** from a hard-wired\n  template (`DEFAULT_CONFIG_TEXT`, the literal text of the file) and\n  prints `generating config file at … …` so the user sees it\n  happen. The hard-wired defaults exist only as that template; the\n  on-disk file is the source of truth on every subsequent boot.\n- Field-by-field merge against the parsed template fills in any\n  fields the user trimmed out of their own config, so partial\n  configs still work.\n- Path resolution: absolute (`/x`) as-is, home-relative (`~/x`)\n  with `$HOME` expansion, project-relative (`x`) prefixed with\n  the project root.\n\nBoot log prints the resolved config so the user can confirm what's\nin effect before troubleshooting.\n\n`src/005-http-server.lua` captures the resolved `bundled_dirs`\nlist into module-level state on `serve(opts)` and merges them into\nthe file-browser dir listing alongside the map's own dirs.\n`resolve_src_dirs` now returns entries tagged with one of three\nkinds (instead of just a path):\n\n- `default` — the map's own `src/` directory, identified by\n  canonical-path comparison so a symlinked `/home/...` mount\n  matches a `/mnt/...` server config.\n- `added` — a directory the user attached to this map via the\n  file browser's `+ library dir` button. Stored in\n  `meta.src_dirs`.\n- `bundled` — an editor-wide default from the config file. Stored\n  in the config, not per-map.\n\n`meta.hidden_bundled_dirs` is a per-map opt-out list: paths listed\nthere are filtered out of the bundled merge so a map can hide one\nof the editor-wide defaults without touching the config file.\n\n`assets/js/007-filebrowser.js` paints the `bundled` tag on\n`default` and `bundled` groups (the \"ships with this map\nautomatically\" set) and leaves `added` groups untagged so the\nuser can tell at a glance what they explicitly attached vs what\nis here by default. Right-click → `hide directory` works on every\ngroup — for `added` and `default` it strips from `meta.src_dirs`,\nfor `bundled` it appends to `meta.hidden_bundled_dirs`. The user\nexperiences a single uniform gesture; the storage split is\ninvisible.\n\nVerified end-to-end via curl against the running server: missing\nconfig triggers generation, malformed config triggers a clear\nerror, hidden_bundled_dirs filters the merge, and the three-way\nkind split surfaces correctly for both maps with and without\n`src_dirs` in their meta. User-verified visually in the editor.",

# Phase 3 Progress

Goal: replace the Lua synchronous runner with a C thread pool runner
that executes box graphs in parallel.

The phase is fully designed; implementation is now underway.
Issues 301–313 are the architectural specification. As each issue
moves into implementation, its row is marked "in progress"; on
completion it's moved to `issues/completed/`.

Implementation began **2026-05-12** with the 309 build scaffolding.

## Issues

| ID  | Title                                                       | Status   |
|-----|-------------------------------------------------------------|----------|
| 301 | thread pool lifecycle and per-worker initialization         | in progress · pool + per-worker init/teardown hooks done |
| 302 | per-task slot store with wire-held references               | in progress · core + large-value heap (variable-size payloads) |
| 303 | language runtime spec (pluggable per-language invocation)   | in progress · registry + per-worker init + pool hook + lang filtering |
| 304 | task dispatch layer (C, replaces synchronous executor)      | in progress · plain + comparator + iterator + randomizer + weighted routing + cell-tagged iterator ordering |
| 305 | C graph loader (replaces `003-loader.lua`)                  | in progress · attach + lang enum + 312 fast-path classification |
| 306 | Lua language spec implementation                            | in progress · init + invoke + teardown + per-worker module cache done |

==================== 230
# 230 — Required inputs, the optional flag, and no-null-from-missing

## Status
open

## Current behavior

A box's input port can be left without a wire and without a literal
value. The phase 2 executor runs the box anyway; the function on
the other end gets `nil` (Lua), unset (Bash), or whatever default
the language hands a missing positional arg. Errors only surface if
the function happens to reference the missing arg in a way the
language can't tolerate (e.g. `string.upper(nil)` in Lua).

This is a class of silent-error bug. The user had a wiring intent,
...
## Intended behavior

Every input port is in one of three states by **compile time**:

1. A wire is connected to it.
2. A literal value is set on it.
3. It carries `optional: true` on the port record.
==================== 231
# 231 — Move per-language signature parsers out of the file browser into `langs/<name>/parser.js`

## Status
open

## Current behavior

`assets/js/007-filebrowser.js` carries language-specific code:
- `parse_lua(content)` — extracts `function M.<name>(<params>)`
  declarations and scans for `return` statements to enumerate
  outputs.
- `parse_bash(content)` — extracts `<name>() {` declarations and
  uses `parse_bash_inputs` to count `$N` references.
- `parse_functions(content, filename)` — dispatches on file
  extension to one of the above.
...
## Intended behavior

Each language directory carries a `parser.js` ES module exporting
a single function:

```js
// langs/<name>/parser.js
==================== 233
# 233 — Unified `routing` schema for branching boxes

## Status
open

## Concept

A "branching box" is a regular call box whose output, after the
function runs, is sent to **one of several** downstream
destinations rather than to all of them. Same function, same
inputs, same output value — only the routing decision differs.
Currently we have two flavors:

- **Comparator**: routes the function's output by comparing it
  to a stored threshold. Three fixed branches (`lt` / `eq` /
...
## Intended behavior

Every call box carries a `routing` field. No implicit defaulting:
plain boxes have `routing: { "kind": "plain" }`, the same way
branched boxes carry their own kind. Explicit and consistent —
no "what if I forget the routing field" surprises at validation
time.
==================== 315
# 315 — Reference-counted compiled-map artifacts

## Status
open

## Current behavior

`scripts/soramech-compile.sh` from issue 309 is destructive at the
top:

```bash
rm -rf "$COMPILED"
mkdir -p "$COMPILED" "$COMPILED/boxes" "$COMPILED/src" ...
```

...
## Intended behavior

A compiled artifact carries its own reference count. Other
programs that depend on a particular build of a map are expected
to *acquire* a reference on startup and *release* it on shutdown.
When `soramech-compile` is asked to rebuild a map that has any
live references, it does NOT clobber the existing directory.
==================== 316
# 316 — Simple test runner script

## Status
open

## Current behavior

`make test` builds and runs every unit-test binary plus the
integration runner. The output is helpful when you want to verify
that everything still passes at the end of a working session, but
it's noisy when you're iterating on one specific change:

- The build phase prints `→ src/foo.c`, `→ src/bar.c`, ... for
  every file that compiles or relinks. On a clean tree that's
  dozens of lines before the first test runs.
...
## Intended behavior

A separate script (`scripts/run-unit-tests.sh`) that runs the
already-built unit-test binaries one by one and reports their
results in a uniform format. No build step. No make. Just:
locate the binaries, run them in turn, capture stdout/stderr,
print a one-line per-binary summary, and at the end print a
==================== 317
# 317 — Language spec JSON bridge for data boxes

## Status
open

## Current behavior

A data box (`kind: data` in box JSON) emits a value the rest of
the graph routes downstream. Today the runner reads the box's
`ref` file as raw bytes and emits them — there's no concept of
"authoring this datum in a particular language using that
language's natural structures." If you want to declare a Lua
table as a data box's value, you either:

- Hand-translate the table to JSON, save it as a `.json` file,
...
## Intended behavior

Every language spec gains two callbacks, declared in
`lang_spec_t` (issue 303):

```c
/* Serialize a native-language value into JSON text.
```

ts: 2026-05-19T10:22:15.891Z

Here's the unstarted set, grouped by area:

**Editor / phase-2 extensions** — fully unstarted, ready for pickup:

| ID | Title | One-line summary |
|---|---|---|
| **229** | `read` / `write` box kinds (language-agnostic IO) | Three box kinds — `call` (existing), `read` (path→contents), `write` (path+value→done). Optional outputs are discarded when no wire. Bigger scope: touches schema, loader, dispatch (phase-3 territory). |
| **230** | Required inputs and the optional flag | Every input must have a wire, a literal, or `optional: true` at compile time. Catches silent-nil bugs where a missing wire produces unset args at runtime. |
| **231** | Move signature parsers into `langs/<name>/parser.js` | Extract `parse_lua` / `parse_bash` out of the file browser into per-language ES modules, so adding a new language doesn't require editing the editor. |
| **233** | Unified routing schema for branching boxes | Every call box carries an explicit `routing` field (plain / comparator / iterator / etc.) instead of inferring the kind from sibling fields. Replaces the current ad-hoc comparator+iterator schemas. |
| **234** | Middle-click duplicates the selected box | Short middle-mouse click on the canvas while a box is selected creates a duplicate with the same ref/fn, literal values, and incoming wires. |
| **236** | Box header = filename, output label = `fn()` | Box header shows the basename of `ref`; output port shows `fn_name()` for `call` boxes. Per-kind defaults for non-call boxes. User label still overrides. |
| **237** | String-manipulation library expansion | Grow `libs/text.lua` with upper/lower/trim/replace/replace_first/contains/starts_with/ends_with/length/substring. |

**Phase-3 runtime** — the other dev's territory; mostly already in flight:

- **301–312, 314** all marked "in progress" — pool, slot store, language specs, dispatch, graph loader, build system, etc.
- **313** is research/blocked (whole-program same-language merge)
- **315** — Reference-counted compiled-map artifacts (don't clobber a build that other tools are using)
- **316** — Simple test runner script (run existing test binaries without rebuilding everything)
- **317** — Language spec JSON bridge for data boxes (per-language to-JSON / from-JSON callbacks, so a Lua data box can author with native tables)

If you want to keep working in the editor, **234** is the smallest visible win, **236** is the most stylistically impactful (every box's header changes), and **237** is pure additive scripting with no UI risk. **229** is biggest because it crosses into the phase-3 runtime — best deferred until the dispatch layer settles.

console.log(`FAIL ${i}: out got=${got} expect=${c.out}`); }\n    else console.log(`  out=${got}`);\n  }\n});\n\n// branch-name output preserved\nconst branch = output_label_text({kind:'comparator'}, {name:'lt'});\nconsole.log(branch === 'lt' ? 'OK branch label preserved' : 'FAIL branch label: ' + branch);\n\nconsole.log(fail === 0 ? '\\nALL PASS' : `\\n${fail} FAIL`);\nprocess.exit(fail);\nEOF\nnode /tmp/test-236.js",

1	# text.lua
2	
3	Text manipulation helpers — concat and split. Pure Lua, no external
4	dependencies. Use as `require("text")` from another Lua function, or
5	copy into a map's `src/` and pick the function via the file browser.
6	
7	Both functions output a single value on the box's output wire (per
8	the single-output rule, issue 218).
9	
10	---
11	
12	## M.concat(sep, ...) → string
13	
14	Joins the variadic text arguments with the given separator.
15	
16	| param | type    | description                                          |
17	|-------|---------|------------------------------------------------------|
18	| sep   | string  | separator inserted between every pair of values     |
19	| ...   | strings | any number of text values to join                    |
20	
21	Returns: a single string with all values concatenated.
22	
23	The signature is variadic so the box's `text` input can be marked
24	variadic in the inspector and grown to any number of slots — see
25	issue 217 part B for the editor mechanics.
26	
27	Edge cases: `sep` may be an empty string. Zero-arg call returns the
28	empty string.
29	
30	---
31	
32	## M.split(text, sep) → table
33	
34	Splits `text` into parts on a literal (non-pattern) separator. Returns
35	a Lua array; the driver shim JSON-encodes it for transport on the
36	single output wire. The downstream box decodes the array.
37	
38	| param | type   | description                                          |
39	|-------|--------|------------------------------------------------------|
40	| text  | string | the input string                                     |
41	| sep   | string | the separator to split on (literal — no pattern matching) |
42	
43	Returns: an array (Lua table with integer keys) of the parts.
44	
45	Edge cases:
46	- `nil` text → empty array
47	- `nil` or empty `sep` → single-element array containing the whole input
48	  (avoids an infinite loop and matches common library behavior)
49	- Separator characters that are also Lua-pattern metacharacters (`.`,
50	  `*`, `[`, etc.) are matched literally — `string.find` is called with
51	  `plain = true`.
52	
```

6	local M = {}
7	
8	-- {{{ M.concat
9	-- Join N text values with a separator. The signature is variadic so the
10	-- box can grow its `text` input without modifying this source — see
11	-- issue 217 part B for the editor-side variadic-input mechanics.
12	function M.concat(sep, ...)
13	    return table.concat({ ... }, sep or "")
14	end
15	-- }}}
16	
17	-- {{{ M.split
18	-- Split a text into parts on a literal (non-pattern) separator. Returns
19	-- a Lua array of the parts; the driver shim encodes it as JSON for
20	-- transport on the single output wire. The downstream box decodes the
21	-- array — there is no fan-out into multiple wires (that would require
22	-- knowing the part count at design time, which we don't).
23	--
24	-- Plain-string match: the separator is escaped before being passed to
25	-- string.find so users don't have to think about Lua patterns.
26	function M.split(text, sep)
27	    if text == nil then return {} end
28	    if sep == nil or sep == "" then
29	        -- splitting on empty separator: return a single-element array
30	        -- with the whole input. Avoids an infinite loop and matches
31	        -- common library behavior for split-on-empty.
32	        return { text }
33	    end
34	
35	    local parts = {}
36	    local plain = true   -- treat sep as a literal string, not a pattern
37	    local pos   = 1
38	
39	    while true do
40	        local s, e = string.find(text, sep, pos, plain)
41	        if s == nil then
42	            parts[#parts + 1] = string.sub(text, pos)
43	            break
44	        end
45	        parts[#parts + 1] = string.sub(text, pos, s - 1)
46	        pos = e + 1
47	    end
48	
49	    return parts
50	end
51	-- }}}
52	
53	return M
54

```
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Lua/LuaJIT language driver for SoraMech.
# Loads a .lua module, calls the named function with JSON-decoded arguments,
# and writes the single return value as a JSON value to stdout.
# Contract: <script> <file-path> <fn-name> <arg-count> [<arg> ...]

DIR="/mnt/mtwo/programs/sora/soramech"

FILE_PATH="${1}"
FN_NAME="${2}"
ARG_COUNT="${3}"
shift 3

if [ -z "${FILE_PATH}" ] || [ -z "${FN_NAME}" ]; then
    echo "lua.sh: missing file-path or fn-name" >&2
    exit 1
fi

if ! command -v luajit > /dev/null 2>&1; then
    echo "lua.sh: luajit not found on PATH" >&2
    exit 1
fi

if [ ! -f "${FILE_PATH}" ]; then
    echo "lua.sh: file not found: ${FILE_PATH}" >&2
    exit 1
fi

# build a JSON array of the raw argument strings for passing into lua
ARGS_JSON="["
SEP=""
for ARG in "$@"; do
    ARGS_JSON="${ARGS_JSON}${SEP}${ARG}"
    SEP=","
done
ARGS_JSON="${ARGS_JSON}]"

# set LUA_PATH so dkjson is findable before the shim runs its first require
export LUA_PATH="${DIR}/libs/?.lua;${LUA_PATH:-;}"

# run luajit with the driver shim inline; the shim loads the module,
# decodes arguments, calls the function, and encodes the result
luajit - "${FILE_PATH}" "${FN_NAME}" "${ARGS_JSON}" << 'LUASHIM'
local json = require("dkjson")

-- read driver arguments
local file_path = arg[1]
local fn_name   = arg[2]
local args_raw  = arg[3]
```

-- add libs and the module's own directory to the path for any further requires
local script_dir = file_path:match("^(.+)/[^/]+$") or "."
package.path = script_dir .. "/?.lua;" ..
               "/mnt/mtwo/programs/sora/soramech/libs/?.lua;" ..
               package.path

-- expose map root as a global so box functions can access data files without
-- needing it wired as an explicit input; convention: src/ is one level below root
SORAMECH_MAP_DIR = script_dir:match("^(.+)/[^/]+$") or script_dir

-- decode args array
local args_table, _, jerr = json.decode(args_raw)
if not args_table then
    io.stderr:write("lua.sh: failed to decode args JSON: " .. tostring(jerr) .. "\n")
    os.exit(1)
end

-- load the module
local ok, mod = pcall(dofile, file_path)
if not ok then
    io.stderr:write("lua.sh: error loading " .. file_path .. ": " .. tostring(mod) .. "\n")
    os.exit(1)
end

-- resolve the function
local fn = mod[fn_name]
if type(fn) ~= "function" then
    io.stderr:write("lua.sh: no public function '" .. fn_name .. "' in " .. file_path .. "\n")
    os.exit(1)
end

-- decode each arg from JSON to lua value; strings are passed as-is
local call_args = {}
for i, raw_arg in ipairs(args_table) do
    local val, _, aerr = json.decode(raw_arg)
    if aerr then
        -- not valid JSON — treat as a plain string
        call_args[i] = raw_arg
    else
        call_args[i] = val
```

  "new_string":"Edge cases:\n- `nil` text → empty array\n- `nil` or empty `sep` → single-element array containing the whole input\n  (avoids an infinite loop and matches common library behavior)\n- Separator characters that are also Lua-pattern metacharacters (`.`,\n  `*`, `[`, etc.) are matched literally — `string.find` is called with\n  `plain = true`.\n\n---\n\n## M.upper(text) → string\n\nUpper-cased copy of `text`. `nil` text returns the empty string.\n\n## M.lower(text) → string\n\nLower-cased copy of `text`. `nil` text returns the empty string.\n\n## M.trim(text) → string\n\nStrips leading and trailing whitespace (the `%s` class — spaces,\ntabs, newlines, etc). Whitespace inside the string is preserved.\n\n## M.replace(text, find, repl) → string\n\nReturns `text` with every occurrence of `find` replaced by `repl`.\nPlain-string match; `find` may contain any characters without\nescaping. Empty `find` returns the text unchanged.\n\n## M.replace_first(text, find, repl) → string\n\nSame as `M.replace` but stops after the first occurrence. Two\nfunctions instead of a flag because the editor has no boolean-port\nprimitive yet — picking by function name is clearer than a third\ninput that takes the string `\"true\"`.\n\n## M.contains(text, needle) → boolean\n\nTrue if `needle` appears anywhere in `text`. Empty needle returns\n**false** (asking \"does it contain nothing?\" is meaningless;\nreturning true would silently short-circuit guards).\n\n## M.starts_with(text, prefix) → boolean\n\nTrue if `text` begins with `prefix`. Empty prefix returns true\n(every string begins with the empty string — matches the\nconventional set-theoretic answer).\n\n## M.ends_with(text, suffix) → boolean\n\nTrue if `text` ends with `suffix`. Empty suffix returns true,\nsame reason as `starts_with`.\n\n## M.length(text) → integer\n\nNumber of bytes in `text` (Lua's `#` operator). For UTF-8 input\nthis is the byte length, not the character count — wide\ncharacters count as multiple bytes.\n\n## M.substring(text, start, len) → string\n\nSlice of `text` starting at 1-indexed `start`. If `len` is\nomitted, takes everything from `start` to the end. Out-of-range\npositions follow `string.sub` semantics — clamped, not errored.",

76	  // -- Pan: middle-click drag, or space + left-click drag.
77	  // Pan math is in screen pixels: pan_{x,y} += mouse_delta_in_screen_pixels.
78	  // The pan anchors record the state at mousedown so each mousemove
79	  // computes the pan from the original anchor, not incrementally — that
80	  // way no error accumulates over a long drag.
81	  let panning          = false;
82	  let pan_anchor_mouse = null;
83	  let pan_anchor_pan   = null;
84	
85	  let space_down = false;
86	  window.addEventListener('keydown', e => { if (e.code === 'Space') space_down = true;  });
87	  window.addEventListener('keyup',   e => { if (e.code === 'Space') space_down = false; });
88	
89	  el.addEventListener('mousedown', e => {
90	    if (e.button === 1 || (e.button === 0 && space_down)) {
91	      panning          = true;
92	      pan_anchor_mouse = mouse_pos(e);
93	      pan_anchor_pan   = { x: pan_x, y: pan_y };
94	      e.preventDefault();
95	    }
96	  });
97	
98	  window.addEventListener('mousemove', e => {
99	    if (!panning) return;
100	    const m = mouse_pos(e);
101	    pan_x = pan_anchor_pan.x + (m.x - pan_anchor_mouse.x);
102	    pan_y = pan_anchor_pan.y + (m.y - pan_anchor_mouse.y);
103	    mark_dirty();
104	  });
105	
106	  window.addEventListener('mouseup', () => { panning = false; });
107	
108	  // -- Zoom: scroll wheel, anchored on the cursor.
109	  // The world point under the cursor stays under the cursor across zoom
110	  // changes. After updating zoom we solve the screen-equation for the
111	  // new pan offset:
112	  //     m.x = w.x * zoom + pan_x   →   pan_x = m.x - w.x * zoom
113	  el.addEventListener('wheel', e => {
114	    e.preventDefault();
115	    // Ignore wheel input while a pan drag is in progress. Middle-click
116	    // pans tend to nudge the wheel as a side effect; without this guard
117	    // the canvas zooms unexpectedly mid-pan.
118	    if (panning) return;
119	
120	    const m = mouse_pos(e);
121	    const w = screen_to_world(m.x, m.y);
122	
123	    // Direction by sign of deltaY: up (negative) zooms in, down zooms out.
124	    const factor   = e.deltaY < 0 ? 1.1 : 0.9;
125	    const new_zoom = Math.max(0.15, Math.min(5, zoom * factor));
126	    zoom = new_zoom;
